# Enterprise Triage Knowledge Base: Clinical Protocols, Security Operations & SLA Matrices

Total semi-structured operational records: 627

## [KB-CLIN-001] STEMI Acute Myocardial Infarction
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Cardiology
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: crushing chest pain, substernal pressure radiating to left arm, diaphoresis, shortness of breath
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Immediate 911 activation, aspirin 325mg non-enteric chewed if no contraindication, immediate transfer to cath lab capable facility.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-001]: STEMI Acute Myocardial Infarction. Domain: Cardiology. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 5 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: crushing chest pain, substernal pressure radiating to left arm, diaphoresis, shortness of breath. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Immediate 911 activation, aspirin 325mg non-enteric chewed if no contraindication, immediate transfer to cath lab capable facility.

## [KB-CLIN-010] Acute Aortic Dissection
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Cardiology
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: tearing chest pain radiating to back, unequal radial pulses, severe acute hypertension, syncope
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Emergency vascular surgical consultation, emergent CT angiography, strict blood pressure control with IV beta blockers.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-010]: Acute Aortic Dissection. Domain: Cardiology. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 5 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: tearing chest pain radiating to back, unequal radial pulses, severe acute hypertension, syncope. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Emergency vascular surgical consultation, emergent CT angiography, strict blood pressure control with IV beta blockers.

## [KB-CLIN-019] Ventricular Fibrillation / Pulseless VT
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Cardiology
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 2 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: sudden cardiac arrest, unresponsive, absent carotid pulse, agonal breathing
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Immediate CPR, chest compressions, AED attachment and defibrillation, call emergency medical services immediately.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-019]: Ventricular Fibrillation / Pulseless VT. Domain: Cardiology. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 2 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: sudden cardiac arrest, unresponsive, absent carotid pulse, agonal breathing. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Immediate CPR, chest compressions, AED attachment and defibrillation, call emergency medical services immediately.

---

## [KB-CLIN-028] Acute Decompensated Heart Failure
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Cardiology
- **Severity Level**: `High` (Target SLA: 15 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: orthopnea, paroxysmal nocturnal dyspnea, bilateral lower extremity edema, pink frothy sputum
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Upright positioning, high-flow supplemental oxygen, IV loop diuretics, immediate telemetry bed placement.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-028]: Acute Decompensated Heart Failure. Domain: Cardiology. Severity: High. Target SLA: 15 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: orthopnea, paroxysmal nocturnal dyspnea, bilateral lower extremity edema, pink frothy sputum. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Upright positioning, high-flow supplemental oxygen, IV loop diuretics, immediate telemetry bed placement.

- **Severity Level**: `High` (Target SLA: 15 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: orthopnea, paroxysmal nocturnal dyspnea, bilateral lower extremity edema, pink frothy sputum, clinical variance marker 6
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Upright positioning, high-flow supplemental oxygen, IV loop diuretics, immediate telemetry bed placement. (Specific differential note for clinical variant 6).
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-034]: Acute Decompensated Heart Failure (Variant 6). Severity: High. SLA: 15m. Red Flags: orthopnea, paroxysmal nocturnal dyspnea, bilateral lower extremity edema, pink frothy sputum. Diagnostic and therapeutic escalation guideline: Upright positioning, high-flow supplemental oxygen, IV loop diuretics, immediate telemetry bed placement. Variant operational modifier 6 applies.

## [KB-CLIN-037] Hypertensive Crisis with End-Organ Damage
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Cardiology
- **Severity Level**: `High` (Target SLA: 15 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: BP > 180/120, acute papilledema, severe occipital headache, microscopic hematuria
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Gradual MAP reduction by 20-25% over 1st hour using IV labetalol or nicardipine, continuous arterial line monitoring.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-037]: Hypertensive Crisis with End-Organ Damage. Domain: Cardiology. Severity: High. Target SLA: 15 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: BP > 180/120, acute papilledema, severe occipital headache, microscopic hematuria. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Gradual MAP reduction by 20-25% over 1st hour using IV labetalol or nicardipine, continuous arterial line monitoring.

## [KB-CLIN-046] Atrial Fibrillation with Rapid Ventricular Response
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Cardiology
- **Severity Level**: `Medium` (Target SLA: 30 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: irregular palpitations, heart rate > 130 bpm, lightheadedness without syncope
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: ECG verification, rate control with diltiazem or metoprolol, assessment of CHA2DS2-VASc score for anticoagulation.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-046]: Atrial Fibrillation with Rapid Ventricular Response. Domain: Cardiology. Severity: Medium. Target SLA: 30 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: irregular palpitations, heart rate > 130 bpm, lightheadedness without syncope. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: ECG verification, rate control with diltiazem or metoprolol, assessment of CHA2DS2-VASc score for anticoagulation.

## [KB-CLIN-055] Stable Angina Pectoris
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Cardiology
- **Severity Level**: `Medium` (Target SLA: 45 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: exertional retrosternal discomfort, relieved by rest or sublingual nitroglycerin within 5 minutes
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Outpatient cardiology referral, stress testing, antianginal optimization, patient education on when to seek ER care.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-055]: Stable Angina Pectoris. Domain: Cardiology. Severity: Medium. Target SLA: 45 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: exertional retrosternal discomfort, relieved by rest or sublingual nitroglycerin within 5 minutes. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Outpatient cardiology referral, stress testing, antianginal optimization, patient education on when to seek ER care.

## [KB-CLIN-064] Benign Premature Ventricular Contractions
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Cardiology
- **Severity Level**: `Low` (Target SLA: 120 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: occasional skipped heartbeats, caffeine-associated palpitations, normal hemodynamics
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Reassurance, 24-hour Holter monitor requisition, electrolytes review, reduction in caffeine and nicotine stimulants.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-064]: Benign Premature Ventricular Contractions. Domain: Cardiology. Severity: Low. Target SLA: 120 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: occasional skipped heartbeats, caffeine-associated palpitations, normal hemodynamics. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Reassurance, 24-hour Holter monitor requisition, electrolytes review, reduction in caffeine and nicotine stimulants.

## [KB-CLIN-073] Essential Hypertension Stage 1 Workup
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Cardiology
- **Severity Level**: `Low` (Target SLA: 240 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: asymptomatic systolic BP 130-139, diastolic BP 80-89, no end-organ damage
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Ambulatory blood pressure monitoring, dietary sodium restriction, lifestyle interventions, routine clinic follow-up.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-073]: Essential Hypertension Stage 1 Workup. Domain: Cardiology. Severity: Low. Target SLA: 240 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: asymptomatic systolic BP 130-139, diastolic BP 80-89, no end-organ damage. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Ambulatory blood pressure monitoring, dietary sodium restriction, lifestyle interventions, routine clinic follow-up.

## [KB-CLIN-082] Acute Ischemic Stroke (FAST Protocol)
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Neurology
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: facial droop, unilateral arm weakness, slurred speech or aphasia, onset < 4.5 hours
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Activate code stroke, urgent non-contrast head CT, assess eligibility for IV thrombolysis (Alteplase/Tenecteplase) or endovascular thrombectomy.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-082]: Acute Ischemic Stroke (FAST Protocol). Domain: Neurology. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 5 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: facial droop, unilateral arm weakness, slurred speech or aphasia, onset < 4.5 hours. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Activate code stroke, urgent non-contrast head CT, assess eligibility for IV thrombolysis (Alteplase/Tenecteplase) or endovascular thrombectomy.

## [KB-CLIN-091] Spontaneous Subarachnoid Hemorrhage
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Neurology
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: thunderclap headache, worst headache of life, meningismus, photophobia, altered mental status
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Emergent non-contrast head CT, lumbar puncture if CT negative >6h, urgent neurosurgical clipping or endovascular coiling.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-091]: Spontaneous Subarachnoid Hemorrhage. Domain: Neurology. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 5 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: thunderclap headache, worst headache of life, meningismus, photophobia, altered mental status. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Emergent non-contrast head CT, lumbar puncture if CT negative >6h, urgent neurosurgical clipping or endovascular coiling.

- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: thunderclap headache, worst headache of life, meningismus, photophobia, altered mental status, clinical variance marker 6
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Emergent non-contrast head CT, lumbar puncture if CT negative >6h, urgent neurosurgical clipping or endovascular coiling. (Specific differential note for clinical variant 6).
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-097]: Spontaneous Subarachnoid Hemorrhage (Variant 6). Severity: Critical. SLA: 5m. Red Flags: thunderclap headache, worst headache of life, meningismus, photophobia, altered mental status. Diagnostic and therapeutic escalation guideline: Emergent non-contrast head CT, lumbar puncture if CT negative >6h, urgent neurosurgical clipping or endovascular coiling. Variant operational modifier 6 applies.

## [KB-CLIN-100] Status Epilepticus
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Neurology
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: continuous seizure activity > 5 minutes, two or more seizures without full recovery of consciousness
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Airway protection, lateral decubitus positioning, IV lorazepam 4mg bolus, followed by IV levetiracetam or fosphenytoin loading.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-100]: Status Epilepticus. Domain: Neurology. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 5 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: continuous seizure activity > 5 minutes, two or more seizures without full recovery of consciousness. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Airway protection, lateral decubitus positioning, IV lorazepam 4mg bolus, followed by IV levetiracetam or fosphenytoin loading.

## [KB-CLIN-109] Acute Bacterial Meningitis
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Neurology
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 10 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: classic triad: high fever, nuchal rigidity, altered mental status, positive Kernig or Brudzinski sign, petechial rash
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Immediate blood cultures, empiric IV ceftriaxone + vancomycin + dexamethasone prior to lumbar puncture if delay anticipated.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-109]: Acute Bacterial Meningitis. Domain: Neurology. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 10 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: classic triad: high fever, nuchal rigidity, altered mental status, positive Kernig or Brudzinski sign, petechial rash. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Immediate blood cultures, empiric IV ceftriaxone + vancomycin + dexamethasone prior to lumbar puncture if delay anticipated.

## [KB-CLIN-118] Transient Ischemic Attack (TIA)
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Neurology
- **Severity Level**: `High` (Target SLA: 15 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: transient focal neurological deficit resolving spontaneously within 1 hour, amaurosis fugax
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Immediate ABCD2 score calculation, urgent MRI brain with DWI, carotid duplex ultrasonography, dual antiplatelet therapy consideration.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-118]: Transient Ischemic Attack (TIA). Domain: Neurology. Severity: High. Target SLA: 15 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: transient focal neurological deficit resolving spontaneously within 1 hour, amaurosis fugax. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Immediate ABCD2 score calculation, urgent MRI brain with DWI, carotid duplex ultrasonography, dual antiplatelet therapy consideration.

---

## [KB-CLIN-127] Complex Migraine with Aura
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Neurology
- **Severity Level**: `Medium` (Target SLA: 60 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: scintillating scotoma, unilateral throbbing headache, nausea, photophobia, prior similar episodes
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Exclude secondary causes, administer oral triptan (e.g., sumatriptan) + NSAID, dark quiet room rest.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-127]: Complex Migraine with Aura. Domain: Neurology. Severity: Medium. Target SLA: 60 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: scintillating scotoma, unilateral throbbing headache, nausea, photophobia, prior similar episodes. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Exclude secondary causes, administer oral triptan (e.g., sumatriptan) + NSAID, dark quiet room rest.

- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Neurology
- **Severity Level**: `Medium` (Target SLA: 60 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: scintillating scotoma, unilateral throbbing headache, nausea, photophobia, prior similar episodes, clinical variance marker 1
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Exclude secondary causes, administer oral triptan (e.g., sumatriptan) + NSAID, dark quiet room rest. (Specific differential note for clinical variant 1).
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-128]: Complex Migraine with Aura (Variant 1). Severity: Medium. SLA: 60m. Red Flags: scintillating scotoma, unilateral throbbing headache, nausea, photophobia, prior similar episodes. Diagnostic and therapeutic escalation guideline: Exclude secondary causes, administer oral triptan (e.g., sumatriptan) + NSAID, dark quiet room rest. Variant operational modifier 1 applies.

## [KB-CLIN-136] Peripheral Vestibular Neuritis / BPPV
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Neurology
- **Severity Level**: `Medium` (Target SLA: 60 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: acute rotational vertigo exacerbated by head movements, horizontal nystagmus, normal HINTS exam
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Dix-Hallpike diagnostic maneuver, Epley canalith repositioning, short-course meclizine or dimenhydrinate.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-136]: Peripheral Vestibular Neuritis / BPPV. Domain: Neurology. Severity: Medium. Target SLA: 60 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: acute rotational vertigo exacerbated by head movements, horizontal nystagmus, normal HINTS exam. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Dix-Hallpike diagnostic maneuver, Epley canalith repositioning, short-course meclizine or dimenhydrinate.

## [KB-CLIN-145] Tension-Type Headache
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Neurology
- **Severity Level**: `Low` (Target SLA: 180 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: band-like bilateral dull pressure, no nausea or vomiting, not worsened by routine physical activity
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Acetaminophen or ibuprofen, hydration, stress reduction techniques, sleep hygiene counseling.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-145]: Tension-Type Headache. Domain: Neurology. Severity: Low. Target SLA: 180 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: band-like bilateral dull pressure, no nausea or vomiting, not worsened by routine physical activity. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Acetaminophen or ibuprofen, hydration, stress reduction techniques, sleep hygiene counseling.

## [KB-CLIN-154] Anaphylactic Shock
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Respiratory & Immunology
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 3 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: stridor, laryngeal edema, diffuse urticaria, hypotension, wheezing after exposure to allergen
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Immediate Intramuscular Epinephrine 0.3-0.5 mg (1:1000) into anterolateral mid-thigh, supine with legs elevated, high-flow O2, IV fluid bolus.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-154]: Anaphylactic Shock. Domain: Respiratory & Immunology. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 3 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: stridor, laryngeal edema, diffuse urticaria, hypotension, wheezing after exposure to allergen. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Immediate Intramuscular Epinephrine 0.3-0.5 mg (1:1000) into anterolateral mid-thigh, supine with legs elevated, high-flow O2, IV fluid bolus.

---

- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Immediate Intramuscular Epinephrine 0.3-0.5 mg (1:1000) into anterolateral mid-thigh, supine with legs elevated, high-flow O2, IV fluid bolus. (Specific differential note for clinical variant 5).
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-159]: Anaphylactic Shock (Variant 5). Severity: Critical. SLA: 3m. Red Flags: stridor, laryngeal edema, diffuse urticaria, hypotension, wheezing after exposure to allergen. Diagnostic and therapeutic escalation guideline: Immediate Intramuscular Epinephrine 0.3-0.5 mg (1:1000) into anterolateral mid-thigh, supine with legs elevated, high-flow O2, IV fluid bolus. Variant operational modifier 5 applies.

## [KB-CLIN-163] Tension Pneumothorax
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Respiratory & Immunology
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: unilateral absent breath sounds, hyperresonance, tracheal deviation away from affected side, severe hypotension
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Immediate needle decompression with 14-gauge angiocatheter in 2nd intercostal space midclavicular line, emergent tube thoracostomy.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-163]: Tension Pneumothorax. Domain: Respiratory & Immunology. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 5 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: unilateral absent breath sounds, hyperresonance, tracheal deviation away from affected side, severe hypotension. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Immediate needle decompression with 14-gauge angiocatheter in 2nd intercostal space midclavicular line, emergent tube thoracostomy.

## [KB-CLIN-172] Severe Acute Asthma Exacerbation
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Respiratory & Immunology
- **Severity Level**: `High` (Target SLA: 15 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: inability to speak in full sentences, accessory muscle use, silent chest, pulsus paradoxus, SpO2 < 90%
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Continuous nebulized albuterol/ipratropium, systemic IV corticosteroids (methylprednisolone), IV magnesium sulfate, ICU escalation.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-172]: Severe Acute Asthma Exacerbation. Domain: Respiratory & Immunology. Severity: High. Target SLA: 15 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: inability to speak in full sentences, accessory muscle use, silent chest, pulsus paradoxus, SpO2 < 90%. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Continuous nebulized albuterol/ipratropium, systemic IV corticosteroids (methylprednisolone), IV magnesium sulfate, ICU escalation.

## [KB-CLIN-181] Acute Pulmonary Embolism
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Respiratory & Immunology
- **Severity Level**: `High` (Target SLA: 15 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: acute pleuritic chest pain, sudden dyspnea, tachycardia > 100 bpm, hemoptysis, unilateral calf swelling
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Wells score calculation, CT pulmonary angiography or D-dimer, therapeutic anticoagulation with LMWH or direct oral anticoagulant.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-181]: Acute Pulmonary Embolism. Domain: Respiratory & Immunology. Severity: High. Target SLA: 15 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: acute pleuritic chest pain, sudden dyspnea, tachycardia > 100 bpm, hemoptysis, unilateral calf swelling. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Wells score calculation, CT pulmonary angiography or D-dimer, therapeutic anticoagulation with LMWH or direct oral anticoagulant.

## [KB-CLIN-189] Acute Pulmonary Embolism - Clinical Guideline Variant 8
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Respiratory & Immunology
- **Severity Level**: `High` (Target SLA: 15 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: acute pleuritic chest pain, sudden dyspnea, tachycardia > 100 bpm, hemoptysis, unilateral calf swelling, clinical variance marker 8
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Wells score calculation, CT pulmonary angiography or D-dimer, therapeutic anticoagulation with LMWH or direct oral anticoagulant. (Specific differential note for clinical variant 8).
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-189]: Acute Pulmonary Embolism (Variant 8). Severity: High. SLA: 15m. Red Flags: acute pleuritic chest pain, sudden dyspnea, tachycardia > 100 bpm, hemoptysis, unilateral calf swelling. Diagnostic and therapeutic escalation guideline: Wells score calculation, CT pulmonary angiography or D-dimer, therapeutic anticoagulation with LMWH or direct oral anticoagulant. Variant operational modifier 8 applies.

## [KB-CLIN-190] Moderate Community-Acquired Pneumonia
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Respiratory & Immunology
- **Severity Level**: `Medium` (Target SLA: 60 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: productive cough with purulent sputum, fever > 38.5C, localized crackles, infiltrate on chest radiograph
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: CURB-65 assessment, empiric oral amoxicillin-clavulanate + azithromycin or doxycycline, outpatient vs inpatient admission decision.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-190]: Moderate Community-Acquired Pneumonia. Domain: Respiratory & Immunology. Severity: Medium. Target SLA: 60 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: productive cough with purulent sputum, fever > 38.5C, localized crackles, infiltrate on chest radiograph. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: CURB-65 assessment, empiric oral amoxicillin-clavulanate + azithromycin or doxycycline, outpatient vs inpatient admission decision.

## [KB-CLIN-199] Mild Seasonal Allergic Rhinitis
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Respiratory & Immunology
- **Severity Level**: `Low` (Target SLA: 240 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: paroxysmal sneezing, clear watery rhinorrhea, nasal pruritus, allergic shiners
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Intranasal fluticasone propionate spray, second-generation oral antihistamine (cetirizine/loratadine), allergen avoidance counseling.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-199]: Mild Seasonal Allergic Rhinitis. Domain: Respiratory & Immunology. Severity: Low. Target SLA: 240 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: paroxysmal sneezing, clear watery rhinorrhea, nasal pruritus, allergic shiners. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Intranasal fluticasone propionate spray, second-generation oral antihistamine (cetirizine/loratadine), allergen avoidance counseling.

## [KB-CLIN-208] Acute Opioid Overdose
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Toxicology & Overdose
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 2 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: pinpoint pupils (miosis), respiratory depression < 8 breaths/min, cyanosis, unresponsive coma
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Immediate Naloxone 0.4-2.0 mg IV/IM or 4 mg intranasal spray, bag-valve-mask ventilatory support, repeat naloxone every 2-3 minutes as needed.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-208]: Acute Opioid Overdose. Domain: Toxicology & Overdose. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 2 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: pinpoint pupils (miosis), respiratory depression < 8 breaths/min, cyanosis, unresponsive coma. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Immediate Naloxone 0.4-2.0 mg IV/IM or 4 mg intranasal spray, bag-valve-mask ventilatory support, repeat naloxone every 2-3 minutes as needed.

- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Toxicology & Overdose

- **Identified Red Flags**: cherry-red lips/skin, altered consciousness, metabolic acidosis with elevated carboxyhemoglobin > 25%, seizure
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Immediate removal from exposure source, 100% normobaric oxygen via non-rebreather mask, urgent hyperbaric oxygen therapy consultation.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-217]: Severe Carbon Monoxide Poisoning. Domain: Toxicology & Overdose. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 10 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: cherry-red lips/skin, altered consciousness, metabolic acidosis with elevated carboxyhemoglobin > 25%, seizure. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Immediate removal from exposure source, 100% normobaric oxygen via non-rebreather mask, urgent hyperbaric oxygen therapy consultation.

## [KB-CLIN-226] Acute Acetaminophen Toxicity
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Toxicology & Overdose
- **Severity Level**: `High` (Target SLA: 20 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: ingestion > 150 mg/kg or 7.5g within 24 hours, initial asymptomatic or mild nausea, toxic Rumack-Matthew nomogram
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Serum acetaminophen level at 4 hours post-ingestion, immediate IV or oral N-acetylcysteine (NAC) protocol before 8 hours post-ingestion.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-226]: Acute Acetaminophen Toxicity. Domain: Toxicology & Overdose. Severity: High. Target SLA: 20 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: ingestion > 150 mg/kg or 7.5g within 24 hours, initial asymptomatic or mild nausea, toxic Rumack-Matthew nomogram. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Serum acetaminophen level at 4 hours post-ingestion, immediate IV or oral N-acetylcysteine (NAC) protocol before 8 hours post-ingestion.

## [KB-CLIN-235] Acute Alcohol Intoxication without Coma
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Toxicology & Overdose
- **Severity Level**: `Medium` (Target SLA: 60 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: slurred speech, ataxia, intoxicated odor, patent airway, arousable
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Monitor airway, check point-of-care capillary glucose to rule out hypoglycemia, IV thiamine 100mg prior to dextrose if malnutrition suspected.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-235]: Acute Alcohol Intoxication without Coma. Domain: Toxicology & Overdose. Severity: Medium. Target SLA: 60 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: slurred speech, ataxia, intoxicated odor, patent airway, arousable. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Monitor airway, check point-of-care capillary glucose to rule out hypoglycemia, IV thiamine 100mg prior to dextrose if malnutrition suspected.

## [KB-CLIN-244] Massive Arterial Hemorrhage
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Trauma & Wounds
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 3 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: pulsatile bright red arterial bleeding from extremity, failing direct pressure, rapid hypovolemic shock
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Immediate application of combat application tourniquet (CAT) 2-3 inches proximal to injury site, tighten until bleeding ceases, log tourniquet time.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-244]: Massive Arterial Hemorrhage. Domain: Trauma & Wounds. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 3 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: pulsatile bright red arterial bleeding from extremity, failing direct pressure, rapid hypovolemic shock. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Immediate application of combat application tourniquet (CAT) 2-3 inches proximal to injury site, tighten until bleeding ceases, log tourniquet time.

## [KB-CLIN-253] Traumatic Open Flail Chest
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Trauma & Wounds
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: paradoxical chest wall movement, multiple contiguous fractured ribs, severe respiratory insufficiency
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Endotracheal intubation with positive pressure ventilation, surgical stabilization of rib fractures, continuous pulse oximetry.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-253]: Traumatic Open Flail Chest. Domain: Trauma & Wounds. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 5 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: paradoxical chest wall movement, multiple contiguous fractured ribs, severe respiratory insufficiency. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Endotracheal intubation with positive pressure ventilation, surgical stabilization of rib fractures, continuous pulse oximetry.

## [KB-CLIN-262] Deep Laceration Requiring Primary Closure
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Trauma & Wounds
- **Severity Level**: `Medium` (Target SLA: 45 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: full-thickness skin laceration through subcutaneous tissue, active capillary ooze, distal neurovascular intact
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Wound irrigation with sterile normal saline, local lidocaine anesthesia, layered primary suture closure within 8-12 hours.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-262]: Deep Laceration Requiring Primary Closure. Domain: Trauma & Wounds. Severity: Medium. Target SLA: 45 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: full-thickness skin laceration through subcutaneous tissue, active capillary ooze, distal neurovascular intact. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Wound irrigation with sterile normal saline, local lidocaine anesthesia, layered primary suture closure within 8-12 hours.

## [KB-CLIN-271] Minor Superficial Epidermal Abrasion
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Trauma & Wounds
- **Severity Level**: `Low` (Target SLA: 240 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: scraped knee/elbow, intact sensation, no retained particulate matter
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Mild soap and water cleansing, topical petrolatum or bacitracin ointment, protective non-stick bandage application.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-271]: Minor Superficial Epidermal Abrasion. Domain: Trauma & Wounds. Severity: Low. Target SLA: 240 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: scraped knee/elbow, intact sensation, no retained particulate matter. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Mild soap and water cleansing, topical petrolatum or bacitracin ointment, protective non-stick bandage application.

## [KB-CLIN-280] Active Suicidal Ideation with Imminent Intent & Lethal Plan
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Psychiatry & Behavioral Health
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 3 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: explicit statement of intent to commit suicide, has firearm or stockpile of pills, hopelessness, saying goodbyes
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Immediate 1-on-1 constant safety observation, remove all lethal means, activate 988 Suicide & Crisis Lifeline / 911 dispatch, transfer to secure psychiatric emergency facility.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-280]: Active Suicidal Ideation with Imminent Intent & Lethal Plan. Domain: Psychiatry & Behavioral Health. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 3 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: explicit statement of intent to commit suicide, has firearm or stockpile of pills, hopelessness, saying goodbyes. ## [KB-CLIN-289] Severe Acute Agitation / Psychosis with Imminent Violence
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Psychiatry & Behavioral Health
- **Severity Level**: `High` (Target SLA: 10 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: command auditory hallucinations, paranoid violent threats, unable to de-escalate verbally
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Verbal de-escalation in low-stimulus environment, physical safety distance, chemical restraint with intramuscular haloperidol + lorazepam.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-289]: Severe Acute Agitation / Psychosis with Imminent Violence. Domain: Psychiatry & Behavioral Health. Severity: High. Target SLA: 10 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: command auditory hallucinations, paranoid violent threats, unable to de-escalate verbally. ## [KB-CLIN-298] Acute Panic Disorder Attack
- **Domain**: Clinical Triage / Psychiatry & Behavioral Health
- **Severity Level**: `Medium` (Target SLA: 45 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: sudden hyperventilation, carpopedal spasm, derealization, fear of impending doom, normal cardiac troponin
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Structured diaphragmatic breathing coaching (4-7-8 method), reassure patient of benign nature, short-acting hydroxyzine or sublingual alprazolam if refractory.
- **Standard Protocol**: CLINICAL PROTOCOL [KB-CLIN-298]: Acute Panic Disorder Attack. Domain: Psychiatry & Behavioral Health. Severity: Medium. Target SLA: 45 minutes. Identified Key Red Flags: sudden hyperventilation, carpopedal spasm, derealization, fear of impending doom, normal cardiac troponin. Mandatory Escalation & Treatment Path: Structured diaphragmatic breathing coaching (4-7-8 method), reassure patient of benign nature, short-acting hydroxyzine or sublingual alprazolam if refractory.

## [KB-SEC-001] Active Credential Stuffing & Automated Brute Force
- **Domain**: Account Security / Identity & Access Control
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: rapid failed login attempts from distributed IPs, credential dumping match, mass account takeover
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Trigger immediate perimeter IP rate-limiting, force global password invalidation, notify account holder via verified SMS/email, enforce biometric re-authentication.
- **Standard Protocol**: SECURITY INCIDENT PROTOCOL [KB-SEC-001]: Active Credential Stuffing & Automated Brute Force. Severity: Critical. SLA: 5m. Red Flags: rapid failed login attempts from distributed IPs, credential dumping match, mass account takeover. Mandatory Containment Workflow: Trigger immediate perimeter IP rate-limiting, force global password invalidation, notify account holder via verified SMS/email, enforce biometric re-authentication.

## [KB-SEC-019] Unauthorized 2FA Device Registration & SIM Swapping
- **Domain**: Account Security / Identity & Access Control
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: out-of-band SMS routing change, new authenticator app registered without approval, sim swap telemetry flag
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Freeze all fund disbursements and sensitive profile edits for 72 hours, revoke active OAuth session tokens, initiate mandatory out-of-band video identity verification.

Red Flags: out-of-band SMS routing change, new authenticator app registered without approval, sim swap telemetry flag. ## [KB-SEC-073] Privileged Cloud IAM Role Escalation
- **Domain**: Account Security / Identity & Access Control
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: service account created with AdministratorAccess, unauthorized assume-role event in AWS/GCP/Azure
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Immediately detach administrative IAM policies, disable access keys, isolate compromised instance role, trigger audit log review.
- **Standard Protocol**: SECURITY INCIDENT PROTOCOL [KB-SEC-073]: Privileged Cloud IAM Role Escalation. Severity: Critical. SLA: 5m. Red Flags: service account created with AdministratorAccess, unauthorized assume-role event in AWS/GCP/Azure. Mandatory Containment Workflow: Immediately detach administrative IAM policies, disable access keys, isolate compromised instance role, trigger audit log review.

## [KB-SEC-109] Repeated Password Lockout on Personal Account
- **Domain**: Account Security / Identity & Access Control
- **Severity Level**: `Medium` (Target SLA: 60 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: 5 consecutive incorrect password entries, account soft-locked for 30 minutes
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Send automated self-service password reset email with secure one-time cryptographic token, unlock upon successful email verification.
- **Standard Protocol**: SECURITY INCIDENT PROTOCOL [KB-SEC-109]: Repeated Password Lockout on Personal Account. Severity: Medium. SLA: 60m. Red Flags: 5 consecutive incorrect password entries, account soft-locked for 30 minutes. Mandatory Containment Workflow: Send automated self-service password reset email with secure one-time cryptographic token, unlock upon successful email verification.

## [KB-SEC-127] Routine Multi-Factor Authenticator App Re-Enrollment
- **Domain**: Account Security / Identity & Access Control
- **Severity Level**: `Low` (Target SLA: 240 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: user purchased new smartphone, wants to migrate Google/Microsoft Authenticator to new hardware
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Guide customer through in-app QR code re-enrollment utilizing existing verified backup codes or SMS secondary fallback.
- **Standard Protocol**: SECURITY INCIDENT PROTOCOL [KB-SEC-127]: Routine Multi-Factor Authenticator App Re-Enrollment. Severity: Low. SLA: 240m. Red Flags: user purchased new smartphone, wants to migrate Google/Microsoft Authenticator to new hardware. ## [KB-FIN-001] Unauthorized International Wire Transfer > $25,000
- **Domain**: Transaction Disputes / Payments & Fraud Ops
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: unauthorized SWIFT wire transfer, funds debited without account holder authorization, international beneficiary entity
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Initiate emergency SWIFT MT199/MT292 wire recall message, freeze sender account to prevent subsequent transfers, contact correspondent bank fraud desk.
- **Standard Protocol**: FINANCIAL DISPUTE PROTOCOL [KB-FIN-001]: Unauthorized International Wire Transfer > $25,000. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 5m. Red Flags: unauthorized SWIFT wire transfer, funds debited without account holder authorization, international beneficiary entity. Mandatory Dispute Resolution Protocol: Initiate emergency SWIFT MT199/MT292 wire recall message, freeze sender account to prevent subsequent transfers, contact correspondent bank fraud desk.

---

- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: unauthorized SWIFT wire transfer, funds debited without account holder authorization, international beneficiary entity, financial discrepancy tier 1
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Initiate emergency SWIFT MT199/MT292 wire recall message, freeze sender account to prevent subsequent transfers, contact correspondent bank fraud desk. Process pursuant to standard dispute regulation annex 1.

- **Identified Red Flags**: unauthorized SWIFT wire transfer, funds debited without account holder authorization, international beneficiary entity, financial discrepancy tier 19
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Initiate emergency SWIFT MT199/MT292 wire recall message, freeze sender account to prevent subsequent transfers, contact correspondent bank fraud desk. Process pursuant to standard dispute regulation annex 19.
- **Standard Protocol**: FINANCIAL DISPUTE PROTOCOL [KB-FIN-020]: Unauthorized International Wire Transfer > $25,000 (Variant 19). Severity: Critical. SLA: 5m. Indicators: unauthorized SWIFT wire transfer, funds debited without account holder authorization, international beneficiary entity. Resolution path: Initiate emergency SWIFT MT199/MT292 wire recall message, freeze sender account to prevent subsequent transfers, contact correspondent bank fraud desk. Annex clause 19.

## [KB-FIN-030] Active Crypto Wallet Drainer Incident
- **Domain**: Transaction Disputes / Payments & Fraud Ops
- **Severity Level**: `Critical` (Target SLA: 5 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: malicious smart contract approval signed, ERC-20 / ETH transfer drain underway, compromised web3 seed phrase
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Advise user to immediately revoke smart contract approvals via Revoke.cash, transfer all remaining uncompromised digital assets to cold wallet, flag hacker address.
- **Standard Protocol**: FINANCIAL DISPUTE PROTOCOL [KB-FIN-030]: Active Crypto Wallet Drainer Incident. Severity: Critical. Target SLA: 5m. Red Flags: malicious smart contract approval signed, ERC-20 / ETH transfer drain underway, compromised web3 seed phrase. Mandatory Dispute Resolution Protocol: Advise user to immediately revoke smart contract approvals via Revoke.cash, transfer all remaining uncompromised digital assets to cold wallet, flag hacker address.

- **Standard Protocol**: FINANCIAL DISPUTE PROTOCOL [KB-FIN-050]: Active Crypto Wallet Drainer Incident (Variant 20). Severity: Critical. SLA: 5m. Indicators: malicious smart contract approval signed, ERC-20 / ETH transfer drain underway, compromised web3 seed phrase. Resolution path: Advise user to immediately revoke smart contract approvals via Revoke.cash, transfer all remaining uncompromised digital assets to cold wallet, flag hacker address. Annex clause 20.

## [KB-FIN-059] ATM Physical Skimming & Serial Cash Withdrawals
- **Domain**: Transaction Disputes / Payments & Fraud Ops
- **Severity Level**: `High` (Target SLA: 15 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: multiple maximum cash withdrawals in minutes, PIN compromise indicator, card present counterfeit duplicate
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Block debit card permanently, issue emergency card replacement, initiate Regulation E provisional credit investigation for all disputed ATM transactions.
- **Standard Protocol**: FINANCIAL DISPUTE PROTOCOL [KB-FIN-059]: ATM Physical Skimming & Serial Cash Withdrawals. Severity: High. Target SLA: 15m. Red Flags: multiple maximum cash withdrawals in minutes, PIN compromise indicator, card present counterfeit duplicate. Mandatory Dispute Resolution Protocol: Block debit card permanently, issue emergency card replacement, initiate Regulation E provisional credit investigation for all disputed ATM transactions.

- **Standard Protocol**: FINANCIAL DISPUTE PROTOCOL [KB-FIN-082]: ATM Physical Skimming & Serial Cash Withdrawals (Variant 23). Severity: High. SLA: 15m. Indicators: multiple maximum cash withdrawals in minutes, PIN compromise indicator, card present counterfeit duplicate. Resolution path: Block debit card permanently, issue emergency card replacement, initiate Regulation E provisional credit investigation for all disputed ATM transactions. Annex clause 23.

## [KB-FIN-088] Unauthorized Recurring ACH Debit (Reg E Dispute)
- **Domain**: Transaction Disputes / Payments & Fraud Ops
- **Severity Level**: `Medium` (Target SLA: 60 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: company debited customer checking account without signed mandate, unauthorized merchant direct debit
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Execute ACH Return Code R10 (Customer Advises Unauthorized), credit customer account within 10 business days per NACHA rules, place stop payment on company ID.
- **Standard Protocol**: FINANCIAL DISPUTE PROTOCOL [KB-FIN-088]: Unauthorized Recurring ACH Debit (Reg E Dispute). Severity: Medium. Target SLA: 60m. Red Flags: company debited customer checking account without signed mandate, unauthorized merchant direct debit. Mandatory Dispute Resolution Protocol: Execute ACH Return Code R10 (Customer Advises Unauthorized), credit customer account within 10 business days per NACHA rules, place stop payment on company ID.

Resolution path: Execute ACH Return Code R10 (Customer Advises Unauthorized), credit customer account within 10 business days per NACHA rules, place stop payment on company ID. Annex clause 24.

## [KB-FIN-117] Double Merchant Billing & POS Duplicate Charge
- **Domain**: Transaction Disputes / Payments & Fraud Ops
- **Severity Level**: `Low` (Target SLA: 240 mins)
- **Identified Red Flags**: single restaurant or grocery checkout charged twice with identical timestamp and merchant ID
- **Clinical / Operational Action**: Request transaction receipt or statement proof, contact acquiring processor for duplicate settlement drop, issue immediate credit if authorization pending.
- **Standard Protocol**: FINANCIAL DISPUTE PROTOCOL [KB-FIN-117]: Double Merchant Billing & POS Duplicate Charge. Severity: Low. Target SLA: 240m. Red Flags: single restaurant or grocery checkout charged twice with identical timestamp and merchant ID. Mandatory Dispute Resolution Protocol: Request transaction receipt or statement proof, contact acquiring processor for duplicate settlement drop, issue immediate credit if authorization pending.

## [KB-SLA-001] SLA-CRITICAL-L1: Immediate Life Safety & Systemic Risk
